2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國聯準會如市場預期 ，維持政策利率不變。 決策官員仍對通膨保持擔憂 ， 也承
認美國經濟正以「強勁速度」成長。聯準會主席Kevin Warsh在會後談話中，並
未明確排除進一步緊縮政策的可能，激勵市場對12 月升息的預期升溫。數據方
面，美國第三季 GDP 年化成長率達 3.4%，高於前值的 2.2%，亦優於市場預期
的 3.2%，印證美國經濟動能強勁。此外，9 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 符合預期，但凸
顯通膨壓力持續偏高。在 「通膨高企」 與 「經濟強韌」 雙重因素驅動下，市場預
期聯準會將於 12 月升息，為美元指數提供有力支撐。惟近期受 AI 產業股票波
動加劇影響，部分資金轉入美債避險，導致美元指數在高檔面臨調節性賣壓。
⚫ 儘管受能源價格走揚影響，9 月歐元區通膨率升至 3.8%，但在薪資壓力維持溫
和，且歐洲央行尚未觀察到第二輪通膨效應全面擴散的情況下 ，歐洲央行上週決
議維持政策利率不變 。通膨回升令市場預期歐洲央行將在下次會議升息 ， 為歐元

提供部分支撐 ， 但考量歐元區經濟成長表現仍落後美國 ， 在美元基本面強勁的抑
制下 ， 歐元反彈力道相對有限 。 目前歐元最大的潛在風險在於部分國家的債券市
場壓力尚未消散，市場對歐洲財政風險仍保持高度警戒。
⚫ 日本央行維持政策利率於 1.25%不變，並且首次明確表示已達成其 2%的通膨目
標；過去日本央行雖認為通膨改善 ， 但始終強調尚未穩定達標，如今首次正式承
認達成 2%目標，代表未來升息門檻進一步下降。此外，日本央行將經濟成長預
測維持在 0.7%至 0.8%，並小幅上修 CPI 預測，令市場預期日央 12 月有望再次
升息，日本貨幣政策正常化將有助日圓結束疲軟走勢。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.390，低點在 32.676，以 32.478 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 3 分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，市場等待聯準會利率決議，交投偏謹慎觀望，而外
資在前周大幅匯出之後，轉為雙向進出，台幣止跌回穩，最高來到 32.390 的周內高
點；然而周間，美國科技股財報表現失色，台股賣壓沉重，外資賣超台股並再度轉
為大幅匯出，台幣走貶，最低來到 32.676，不過匯價逢高吸引出口商賣壓出籠，加
上央行提供流動性，台幣貶幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.478。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣偏弱震盪。就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持利率不
變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，且聯準會表示通膨仍處於高檔，顯示聯準會對通
膨仍保持警戒，市場預期美國利率將維持高檔，台美利差仍維持在相對高位，不利
台幣表現；另一方面，美國科技股財報表現不如預期，引發市場對AI 投資回報的疑
慮，對台股也帶來壓力。就台灣市場來看，外資持續調節台股並大幅匯出，10 月累
計賣超將近兆元，資金匯出壓力大，不過一方面央行持續進場調節，加上匯價逢高
預計吸引出口商進場拋匯，料將限制台幣貶值速度。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢
偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣持續走升。周初市場等待美國聯準會利率決議，交投偏向觀望，境
外人民幣主要於 6.6600 上方震盪； 周間聯準會如預期維持利率不變 ，美元指數自 102
上方回落 ， 加上中國出口結匯需求持續提供支撐， 境外人民幣突破6.6600 整數關口；
週五人行中間價續創近三年九個月新高，最高來到 6.7138，顯示中國人行允許人民
幣溫和升值的走勢，境外人民幣一度升破 6.6500 整數關口，最高來到 6.6487，終場
收在 6.6528，週線升值 162 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6558，週線升值 146 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升 。 就中國國內市場來看 ，10 月官方製造業 PMI 降至 49.3，
重新落入景氣收縮區間，反映中國內需及製造業動能仍有待改善，不過 9 月出口年
增 36.3%，出口商結匯需求持續提供人民幣支撐，加上人行中間價續創近三年九個
月新高，預計將持續帶動人民幣走升；就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持
利率不變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，顯示聯準會對通膨仍保持警戒，市場預期
美國利率將維持高檔 ， 美中利差維持在相對高位 ，將限制人民幣升值的速度與幅度 ；
最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散 ，匯價維持在五日均
線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣
偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6200-6.6700

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 上漲。週初，美伊衝突仍陷僵局支撐美元避險買盤，USD/JPY 最
高來到 158.34；週間，聯準會公布利率維持利率不變，USD/JPY 持續在 158 附近盤
整；臨近週末，日本央行公布利率維持不變並釋放偏鷹訊號， USD/JPY 最低來到
156.94，終場收在 157.53，週線上漲 72 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於上周五宣布利
率維持在 1.25%不變，並表示隨著基礎通膨率大致達到 2%，央行必須持續控制通
膨穩定於此目標，且再次強調貨幣政策已進入更為緊縮階段，未來每隔數月即可能
升息。此外，日本央行在季度報告中，亦將截至 2028 年 3 月財年的核心消費者通
膨預估自三個月前預測的 2.4%上調至 2.6%。日本央行偏鷹立場令市場預期日本最
快將於 12 月再次升息，預計將有利日圓表現。二、聯準會宣布維持利率於 3.75%-
4%區間不變，惟因通膨仍處高位，聯準會重申將致力實現 2%的長期通膨區間， 
FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機率約七成，美元持穩，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空
間。三、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 各天期均線趨平，近期匯價大致
在 156 至 158 間震盪，匯價暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現
區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：156.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.63 158.34

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美伊衝突持續以及法國政經情勢動盪等情況下，
歐元走跌，最低來到 1.1111；週間，聯準會和歐洲央行相繼公布利率決議均維持不
變，歐洲央行發表言論偏鷹派，歐元止跌回升至 1.1214；臨近週末，歐元區公布經
濟數據好壞參半，歐元自高點回落，終場收在 1.1158，週線下跌 41 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行如市場預期宣布
存款利率維持在 2.5%不變，並警告能源成本持續上漲將對通膨造成上行風險，暗示
可能在 12 月再次升息 ， 升息預期升溫預計將支撐歐元走勢 。二 、 歐元區第三季GDP
年增 1.3%， 高於市場預期的1.1%， 顯示在能源價格持續上漲的不利因素下 ，經濟仍
展現韌性，預計有利歐元表現。三、聯準會宣布維持利率在 3.75%-4%不變，並重申
致力控制通膨的立場 。 此外 ，美國公布第三季 GDP 成長 3.4%， 優於市場預期的3.2%，
9 月核心 PCE 年增率自前月的 3%上升至 3.1%，在美國經濟強勁及通膨仍高的情況
下 ， 市場預期聯準會將於 12 月升息一碼，美元持穩 ，預計將限縮歐元走升空間 。四、
技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，不過近期匯價在多次向
下測試 1.1120 附近均未有效跌破 ， 顯示下方仍有買盤支撐。綜合以上 ， 預估EUR/USD
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1050-1.1270 
前週交易區間 
1.1103 1.1226

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.27-115.10.30 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109R 2.03% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.845%~1.846%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 時序邁入月底 ， 銀行間已大致調整好積數 ， 且央行未明顯
增發定存單，令市場資金充裕。近兩日股市回落，令週五資金需求上揚，惟供
需尚屬平衡，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.825% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 本週台債市場交投依舊清淡 ，10 年期指標券 A15109R 等殖系統成交在 2.03%，
5 年期指標券 A15108 議價成交在 1.845%~1.846%。 
美債利率自波段高點回落，且央行總裁楊金龍本週立法院發言維持近來對通
膨容忍度變寬之態度 ， 惟市場對央行升息預期並未完全消除 ， 買盤追價動力有
限，預期台債殖利率維持區間整理，關注下週30 年期公債標售果，預估 5 年
券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券區間為 1.99%~2.04%。

## Document 072: 2026年9月29日國際經濟金融週報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月29日國際經濟金融週報.pdf
Report source: Mega Bank (國際經濟金融週報)
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
中華民國一一五年九月二十九日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和訊號 
經濟 
⚫ S&P Global 公布，9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數(PMI Composite Output 
Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4，創逾 5 年新高；服務業及製造業
PMI 分別升至 58.7 及 57.0。在內需強勁帶動下，新訂單與就業同步成
長，惟供應鏈瓶頸及能源成本攀升推升通膨壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國新屋銷售年化率為 68.4 萬戶，月增 6.4%、年減 2.0%，顯示銷
售較前月回升但需求尚未完全恢復。中西部與南部為主要支撐，惟房價
年減、庫存仍維持 48 萬戶以上，供給相對充裕，後續銷售能否延續仍
待觀察。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.1，月減 7.0%、年減 12.7%，創近四個
月低點。當前狀況與預期指數同步下滑，反映高物價、燃料價格與貿易
不確定性持續壓抑信心；同時通膨預期升溫，後續消費動能仍待觀察。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國國家主席習近平於 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪美並與川普舉行峰會 ， 雙方
同意延長貿易休兵至 2027 年 1 月 10 日，推動部分商品降稅、建立 AI
對話機制及持續磋商稀土供應議題，並延續高層溝通。台灣議題未列入
成果清單，關稅、科技競爭及供應鏈等核心分歧仍未化解，後續談判發
展仍待觀察。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 9 月 PMI 強勁擴張，顯示企業活動與就業動能仍具韌性，但投入
成本與售價上升也反映通膨壓力升溫。相較之下，房市需求尚未全面恢
復，消費信心則明顯轉弱，顯示內需前景仍有隱憂；美中延長貿易休兵
雖有助穩定市場，但核心分歧未解，後續不確定性仍高。 
歐 
元 
區 
9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，服務業 PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，製造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7。 
短評 
⚫ 儘管 9 月經濟表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲
央行的鷹派態度，使得 10 月升息一碼的可能性些微增加，亦強化未來
升息預期。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處

一、 美國—PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和
訊號 
標普全球 (S&P Global) 公布， 2026 年 9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數 (PMI 
Composite Output Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4， 連續第四個月攀升 ， 創2021
年 7 月以來新高；其中服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)由 56.5 升至 58.7，創
59 個月高點，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 由 53.9 升至 57.0，創
2022 年 5 月以來最高水準，顯示美國企業活動持續強勁擴張。受國內市場需求穩
健帶動，製造業與服務業新訂單同步加速成長，推升整體產出增速升至逾 5 年來
最快水準，企業亦積極擴增人力，就業成長速度創 2022 年 6 月以來新高。另一
方面，積壓訂單增加及供應鏈延遲加劇，反映產能利用率持續攀升；加以近期油
價上漲推升燃料及運輸成本，使企業投入成本增幅升至 2022 年 10 月以來最高，
帶動售價漲幅回升，顯示通膨壓力有所升溫。儘管如此，企業對未來一年營運前
景仍維持正向看法，反映市場對美國經濟韌性及需求持續擴張仍具信心。 
2026 年 8 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 68.4 萬戶 ， 較上月增加6.4%，
但較去年同期下降 2.0%，顯示新屋銷售較前月回升，惟仍低於去年同期水準。地
區別表現分歧，其中中西部及南部分別較上月增加 84.9%及 6.9%，東北部及西部
則分別減少 36.1%及 15.2%。價格方面，8 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.37 萬美元，較
上月小升 0.4%，但較去年同期下降 5.8%。供給方面，8 月底經季節性調整後待售
新屋數量為 48.3 萬戶，與上月持平，較去年同期減少 2.0%；按目前銷售速度估
算，供給月數為 8.5 個月，低於上月的 9.0 個月，與去年同期大致持平。整體而
言，8 月美國新屋銷售較前月回升，主要受中西部與南部銷售增加帶動，惟仍低
於去年同期，顯示需求動能尚未完全恢復。價格方面，中位價較去年同期下滑，
反映市場價格仍有調整壓力；同時待售新屋維持在 48 萬戶以上，供給月數仍達
8.5 個月，顯示整體供給相對充裕，後續仍須觀察銷售回升是否具持續性。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 9 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.1，低於上月
的 51.7， 月減7.0%、 年減12.7%， 為近四個月低點 ， 並較2026 年 1 月下降約 15%，
顯示消費者信心持續轉弱。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由51.9 降至 50.9，月減

反映民眾對未來景氣與個人財務前景的看法明顯惡化。調查指出，消費者對高物
價的擔憂持續升高，當前與未來一年個人財務看法均較上月轉弱約一成；短期企
業展望也因燃料價格偏高與貿易爭端再度升溫而明顯下滑。政治立場方面，各陣
營受訪者普遍認為年初以來經濟前景轉弱，其中共和黨人信心較 1 月下降 20%，
民主黨人下降 13%。 通膨預期方面 ， 未來一年通膨預期由8 月的 4.0%升至 4.6%，
為今年 6 月以來最高，且高於今年 2 月的 3.4%(美伊爆發衝突之前)及 2024 年各
月水準；長期通膨預期亦由 3.3%升至 3.4%，結束連續三個月持平，並高於 2024
年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，9 月美國消費信心再度降溫，顯示高物價、燃
料價格與貿易不確定性持續壓抑民眾信心，後續仍須觀察通膨預期升溫是否進一
步影響消費動能。 
中國國家主席習近平於 2026 年 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪問美國，並於 9 月 24 日
在華府白宮與美國總統川普舉行峰會。會後，美中雙方未發布聯合聲明，而是分
別公布成果清單，顯示雙方在穩定雙邊關係及維持高層溝通方面取得部分共識，
包括同意建構「基於尊重、公平、對等的建設性戰略穩定關係」、推動 300 億美
元非敏感商品對等降稅安排、建立人工智慧 (AI)或「超級智慧」對話與風險溝通
機制，以及持續就稀土與關鍵礦產供應鏈議題進行磋商。此外，美方並表示中國
將於 2027 年及 2028 年分別進口至少 1,000 萬公噸美國煤炭。值得注意的是，台
灣議題並未出現在雙方公布的成果文件中；另一方面，中方則額外強調兩軍危機
溝通機制及合作搜尋失蹤美軍遺骸等事項。整體而言，本次川習會有助延續美中
對話與降低短期緊張情勢，惟雙方在關稅、科技競爭、供應鏈安全及其他戰略議
題上的結構性分歧仍未完全化解，後續談判進展及雙邊互動方向仍有待持續觀察。 
二、歐元區—9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，且為連續四個月上
升。其中服務業PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，創近 10 個月高點，主要反映 AI 與國防投資
活動增加，帶動商業服務需求，抵銷能源價格上漲對消費購買力的負面影響。製
造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7，不過，德國萊茵河水位下降，沿岸較為發達的化工、製
藥業物流成本增加，因而暫時壓抑製造業上行空間。值得注意的是，由於國際原
油價格回升，綜合投入、產出物價指數皆上升至近 4 個月最高。故儘管 9 月經濟
表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲央行的鷹派態度，使得

三、日本—9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性 
 日本 9 月綜合 PMI 由 53.5 降至 52.5，製造業與服務業同步回落，經濟成長動
能較前兩個月的高檔有所降溫。製造業 PMI 下跌 0.8 點至 54.1，雖然產出、新訂
單及出口訂單增速放緩，但外部需求仍相當強勁，尤其 AI 相關需求可能持續帶
動出口表現，加上企業開始重建庫存、供應鏈瓶頸尚未完全解除，預料補庫存需
求將在未來數月繼續支撐生產成長。服務業 PMI 下降 0.9 點至 51.6，受到工資、
運輸及能源成本上升影響 ， 需求動能略有降溫 ， 但積壓訂單與出口業務有所改善 。
另，投入成本與售價漲幅均較前期放緩，顯示通膨壓力正逐步緩解，但整體物價
水準仍維持高檔。此外，企業對未來景氣的信心持續改善，未來活動指數升至七
個月高點，反映企業普遍預期經濟擴張趨勢仍可望延續。 
四、中國—美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-7 月)略增 0.1 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 1.9 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 31.1%、71.8%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增20.1%、109.9%)、化學原料
及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.1%、51%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.5%、2.9%，但獲利分別年減 5.2%、16%。非金屬礦物製品業營
收、獲利下滑 9.5、46.7%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減 1.1%、62.4%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，
以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或
政府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
展望未來，儘管美中雙方互相降低等值 300 億美元產品關稅，惟考量雙方每
年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程設備、電動車、
軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，因此實質效益有限。另外，雖然美中雙方同意延長貿
易休戰，但休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給予中國稀土
供應履約的壓力。後續將關注美中能否在休戰期間就開放市場、科技競爭、供應

五、印度及東南亞—亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
亞 洲 開 發 銀 行 (ADB) 最 新 經 濟 展 望 (Asian Development Outlook, ADO 
September 2026 )上修 2026 年亞洲及太平洋地區經濟體 2026 年經濟成長率(GDP)
至 4.7%(7 月預估 4.5%)、2027 年為 4.5%。ADB 認為，亞洲開發中經濟體雖面臨
中東衝突延長、俄烏戰爭再升溫、能源價格高檔震盪及強烈聖嬰現象等逆風，但
在政府刺激政策、民間投資及 AI 帶動科技出口支撐下，區域經濟仍展現韌性。
通膨方面，2026 年通膨率下修至 3.9%，惟能源與糧食價格壓力仍高，2027 年通
膨預測上修至 3.3%。 
區域經濟表現方面 ， 南亞成長最強勁 ，2026 年 GDP 成長率預估上修至 6.4%，
主要受印度內需與投資帶動；東亞則受中國內需疲弱影響，成長維持平穩；東協
國家(ASEAN)上調至 4.7%， 其中越南 、 馬來西亞及泰國受惠於科技與製造業投資
而獲上修。中亞及西亞、太平洋地區則面臨能源及食品價格上漲壓力，成長預測
略為下修至 3.7%、3.0%。(詳下表) 
ADB 認為未來最大風險包括地緣政治衝突升級、聖嬰 現象造成糧食供應衝
擊，以及 AI 相關資產可能出現價格重估。整體而言，AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞
洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨
與信用風險的重要變數。

五、台灣—AI 浪潮推升接單動能，外銷訂單再創新高 
8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，首度突破千億美元大關，其中受惠於 ASIC
晶片需求帶動，資通產品接單翻倍、電子產品接單亦大增 83.9%，惟光學器材因
面板需求維持低檔，年減 5%。非科技產品方面，基本金屬年增 26.8%，係因銅箔
及銅箔基板受惠 AI 相關供應鏈需求及銅價續漲，加以鍍鋅鋼需求回溫；機械年
增 28.3%，係因半導體業者增加資本支出，帶動半導體設備、自動化設備需求延
續；化學品年增 15.4%，主因藥品接單成長，加上產業用化學品需求持續增加；
塑橡膠製品年增 6.7%， 主係塑膠原料受原料上漲而推升產品售價 ， 加上部分塑膠
製品訂單回溫。展望未來，經濟部統計處預估 2026 年外銷訂單有望突破 1 兆美
元，在 AI 相關需求持續擴張帶動下，科技產品表現可望維持強勁，而傳統產業
中下游產品亦可望受惠於相關供應鏈需求，惟上游原物料價格競爭激烈，後續仍
須關注第四季消費旺季是否有相關備貨需求。

### Page 6

Realtek (2379.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Realtek
Company description
Founded in 1987, Realtek is a leading fabless semiconductor with
headquarters in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It has an extensive global footprint with
operational offices in China, Japan, Korea, Singapore, and the US. Realtek's
product portfolio is well-diversified; generally, it can be categorized into three
business groups: Communications Network (Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, automotive
Ethernet, PC Ethernet, switch, etc.), Computer Peripherals (audio codec,

clock IC, card reader, IP cam SoC, USB  hub controller, Type-C controller, SSD
controller) and Multimedia (TV SoC, LCD controller, STB SoC, etc.).
Investment strategy
We rate the shares Buy. We believe Realtek is well-positioned to ride on
continued specs upgrade demand for WiFi, switch and 10G PON as well as the
strong advancements in the automotive and Io T sectors. In the long term, we
believe Realtek's cost-effective, high-performance, and comprehensive wired

and wireless solutions should become more critical with the growing
connectivity requirement at the edge.
Valuation
Our target price for Realtek is set at NT$900 based on our target PE multiple
of 20x to our 2027 EPS. We believe our PER of 20x, higher than its 3-year
forward PER average, is justified by its solid growth opportunity in automotive
and switch/PON business. We also believe its continued efforts in products
mix optimization should help to support its margins expansion. At our target

price, the shares would trade at a 2026/27E P/B of 8x/6x.
Risks
Key downside risks to our target price include: 1) Slowdown in technology
migration for its networking business; 2) Weaker-than-expected growth
momentum for automotive Ethernet sales; 3) Worse-than-expected PC/NB
and consumer electronic demand; 4) Slower-than-expected GM expansion
due to pricing competition from peers; and 5) Higher-than-expected memory
cost burden to dampen the order outlook from its networking and PC/NB

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 109: 261029_daiwa_RTK

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_daiwa_RTK.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: On 29 October, Realtek reported 3Q26 results that missed our estimates but 
were in line with the consensus. This was mainly due to weaker-than-expected revenue 
likely on a deeper demand rebalancing despite pricing power on supply-chain cost 
passthrough. While 4Q26 outlook looks intact, management expects steady topline growth 
into 2027, supported by a multi-year bandwidth upgrade cycle, continued pricing resilience 
and emerging AI opportunities from cloud to edge to physical AI, despite likely still subdued 
PC and consumer electronics demand. We rate the stock Outperform (2) premised upon 
our key thesis of a multi-year/multi-G (gigabits per second) bandwidth upgrade cycle 
across wireline and wireless to benefit Realtek, which is further echoed by management’s 
2027 outlook.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 results miss on lower topline. Realtek reported 3Q26 net profit of TWD4.1bn, 
missing our estimate by 7% while in line with the consensus. With financial metrics such 
as margins broadly in line, the miss was mainly due to lower-than-expected topline 
which came in 8% below our estimate at TWD37bn. We believe the weak revenue was 
driven by a deeper demand rebalancing after customer restocking and pre-stocking in 
1H26 on seasonality and supply-chain uncertainties, flagged in our note, Downgrading: 
pricing to bridge rebalancing but …, 4 August. Although Realtek raised prices to pass on 
cost inflation to the supply chain, it failed to offset such rebalancing. As usual, 
management gave no quantitative guidance for 4Q26 but expressing “prudent and 
steady” revenue run rates, in line with our expectations. We will be reviewing our model 
in due course.

• Key takeaways. At today’s results call, management further expected a steady topline 
growth heading into 2027, driven by: 1) multiple-G bandwidth upgrade cycles across 
wireline and wireless networking, including Wi-Fi 7 and 8, multi-G Ethernet, PON and 
switch, to enhance product mix with market-share gains adding to upside; 2) likely 
sustained pricing power with the company continuing to pass on inflated costs to the 
supply chain; and 3) despite likely still subdued demand from PC and consumer 
electronics markets, the ongoing development of AI, from cloud to edge AI to physical AI 
applications, is creating growth opportunities and tallying with our investment thesis of 
the multi-year/multi-G bandwidth upgrade cycle to structurally benefit Realtek.

Recommendation 
We rate Realtek Outperform (2). The stock price has generally consolidated around 
TWD670-790 over the past 3 months due to lack of catalysts amid its demand rebalancing, 
suggesting patience for investors. Key downside risk to our call would be any commodity 
price hikes leading to worse-than-expected demand rebalancing.

Country

29 October 2026

 Downbeat 3Q26 results yet resilient 2027 guidance

## Document 110: 261029_daiwa_Win Semiconductors

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_daiwa_Win Semiconductors.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following our results memo, we raise our forecasts and TP 
for Win Semiconductors (WinSemi), but lower our rating by one notch to 
Outperform (2) on valuation grounds. While its topline trajectory appears to 
be unfolding intact, its margin trajectory is running better than our 
expectations. This, together with management’s positive commentary 
across its key business lines of RF, infrastructure and optics, reinforces our 
thesis that 3 growth pillars will turn around WinSemi’s bottom line and 
optimise its structural profitability (Upgrading: profit optimisation round the 
corner, 24 July). We suggest accumulating only on pullbacks.

What’s the impact: Forecast revisions. We raise our 2027-28E EPS by 
2-11% for WinSemi to factor in its higher margin trajectory, primarily on 
better product mix geared towards the infrastructure business, followed by 
the ramp-up in optics for AI optical communications (OC). We note the 
surge in our 2026E EPS revision is a result of the company’s non-operating 
gains of TWD684m in 3Q26, mainly from its 23%-owned ITEQ (6213 TT, 
TWD778, Buy [1], covered by Sheng Cheng), which distorts WinSemi’s 
growth trajectory at the bottom line into 2027.

Transformation paying off. At its results call, management commented on 
resilient RF demand from iOS and for WiFi routers, strong demand for low 
Earth orbit (LEO) satellite communications, and a continued ramp in pin 
diode (PD) sales after the product entered mass-production from late-
2Q26. This should be followed by laser diode (LD), such as continuous 
wave (CW) and electro-absorption modulated laser (EML), though 
customer qualifications will take time. All the commentary reinforces our 
thesis that 3 growth pillars will help turn around WinSemi’s bottom line for 
profitability optimisation after its business transformation: 1) RF business 
transformation paying off with premiumisation across cellular and WiFi for 
differentiation, 2) high-margin infrastructure scaling up on a robust LEO 
build, and 3) AI OC ramping fast, with broadened offerings from peripheral 
chips (optical drivers and modulators) to core chips for PD and LD.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we lift our 12M TP for 
WinSemi to TWD620 (previous: TWD449), based on a higher 4-quarter 
forward PER target of 55x (previous: 50x), justified by its 2025-28E EPS 
CAGR of 54% (previous: 50%) from a PEG perspective. Yet we lower our 
rating by one notch to Outperform (2) on valuation grounds, with key 
downside risk of commodity cost hikes worsening consumer demand.

How we differ: Our 2026E EPS is 40% above consensus due to our 
inclusion of the non-operating gains in 3Q26, while our 2027E EPS is 9% 
above likely on our higher margin assumptions.

29 October 2026

Downgrading: profit optimisation on the go

➢ Raising our forecasts on a higher margin trajectory 
➢ Growth pillars paying off across infrastructure, optics and RF 
➢ Downgrading our rating to Outperform (2), with a higher TP of TWD620

Company profile 
Founded in 1999, Win Semiconductors Corp (WinSemi) is the world’s largest compound semiconductor 
foundry, focusing on gallium-arsenide (GaAs) foundry services for customers in both wireless and wire-
line communication markets, as well as infrastructure applications. It has a diverse technology portfolio 
of processes that supports microwave frequency requirements from 50MHz to 100GHz, with 
applications including smartphones, tablet PCs, cellular base-stations, very small aperture terminals 
(VSAT) and fibre optics. It has started penetrating into vertical cavity surface emitting laser (VCSEL) 
foundries since 2017 for consumer laser applications such as 3D laser sensors and cameras.

### Page 5

5

Win Semiconductors (3105 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 4 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
WinSemi's board of directors (BoD) has 10 members, including 3 independent directors,
representing 30% of total directors. Among the BoD, three of its non-independent directors are
also executive management.
Capital management 2
Since 2017, its dividend payout ratio has remained above 58%, except for 2024 when no
dividend was paid. Despite being the largest compound semiconductor foundry in the world,
WinSemi is continually expanding its capacity for future growth. Thus, we see the balance
between investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 It did not have any meaningful related-party transactions in 2023.
S Supply chain management 2
WinSemi We actively cooperate with local suppliers and improve their development capabilities,
reduce carbon emissions and create local employment opportunities. WinSemi also conducts on-
site audits of suppliers and annual assessment of suppliers on environmental protection and
safety & health management systems. In 2023, 100% of WinSemi's new suppliers signed the
"WIN Supplier Code of Conduct".'"
E Water & wastewater management 1
Effluent that flows out of WinSemi’s facilities will first undergo pre-treatment in-house and then
treatment by the wastewater treatment plant at Hwaya Technology Park before discharge. The
plant has an acid and alkali wastewater recycling system and chemical mechanical polishing
wastewater recycling system for wastewater management.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
WinSemi has established a hazardous substance-free management procedure and also
collaborates with its suppliers to produce products that are hazardous substance-free for
customers and consumers. WinSemi specifies in purchase contracts and order forms with its
suppliers that the provided materials may not contain hazardous substances as defined by
WinSemi.
For waste management, it aims to increase the recycling rate of waste solvents and reduce the
use of raw materials in production.
E GHG emissions 1
For reduction of energy consumption, it prioritises high-efficiency and low energy consumption
models when replacing equipment and has also established annual energy conservation
management objectives. WinSemi targets to reduce electricity consumption (kWh/6"e wafer) by
12% from 2018 to 458.58 and PFCs emission (ton CO2e/6"e wafer) by 50% from 2018 to 0.114
in 2030.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Win Semi’s 3Q26 core business (operating income) was 49/41% 
higher than GSe/BBG consensus, mainly driven by a more favorable 
product mix with higher contribution from the high margin 
infrastructure business (accounted for 39% of total revenue in 3Q26 
vs. 34% in 2Q26), resulting in a 9.2ppt QoQ increase in 3Q26 GPM, 
and came in 6.9/6.1ppt above GSe/BBG consensus. Win Semi’s 
OPEX increased to NT$775mn (vs. NT$743mn in 2Q26). NI was 
67/110% higher than GSe/BBG consensus due to the high 
non-operating income of NT$698mn (account for 33% of total 
pretax income). 
For 4Q26, the company expects revenue to increase by low single 
digit QoQ, and expects cellular to see QoQ decline due to weak 
season while the remaining businesses (including 
infrastructure/Wi-Fi/optical) will see flattish to small QoQ increase. 
For GM, the company guides the 4Q26 level to maintain at 
high-thirties %, which is much better than both our prior 
expectation and BBG consensus at 31.8/31.4%. Management 
indicated 3Q26 UTR to be higher at 75% (was 65% in 2Q26), and 
expect to maintain at similar level in 4Q26.  
The company continues to highlight the progress with its AI-related 
business, with its new PD (photodiodes) products started to ramp 
up from 2H26, with more contribution expected in 2027 along with 
more PD capacity coming online. For the new LD (laser diodes) 
products, the company continues to work with multiple clients on 
multiple new solutions (including CW laser, EML solutions and 
VCSEL), but expects to see more contribution in 2028 due to the 
lengthy reliability tests (takes 6M+ per our estimates). The company 
reiterated that CAPEX this year is project driven, which will mainly 
focus on expanding InP and GaN capacity for the datacom optical 
and infrastructure business.

### Page 3

Upward revision to PA foundry TAM reflecting foundry continue to gain
share despite weak smartphone shipment
We have revised up our 2026/27/28E P A foundry TAM by 5/4/2% to
US$1.3bn/1.5bn/1.6bn despite smartphone shipment remaining weak (GSe now at
-10%/+2%/+2% in 2026/27/28, was -10%/+3%/+1% previously, see here) as the
smartphone market remains exposed to the rising memory cost environment (see here).
The upward revision is based on our expectation that PA foundry house will continue to

gain share in the smartphone PA segment as newer smartphone projects from design
houses are being allocated to foundries due to the higher production costs and
increasing design complexity of the PA. This will benefit Win Semi in the mid to long term
(Win Semi’s revenue from cellular business already saw 28% YoY YTD). We now expect
the market TAM to deliver an 8% CAGR over 2026-30E, and we expect foundries’ cellular
PA shipments to increase by 11% in 2026 (Exhibit 2), outperforming our 2026

smartphone shipment estimates of -10% YoY due to the higher than expected PA
foundry penetration (Exhibit 5).
Win Semi delivered 3Q26 revenue that is better than the company’s guidance (actual
QoQ at 31% vs. guidance of low-teens QoQ), with the cellular business in peak season.
Management expects this momentum to slow down in 4Q26 and guided revenue to see
QoQ decline in the cellular business. We now expect Win Semi’s PA foundry revenue to
grow 31% YoY in 2026 (was 28% previously), outpacing the overall PA foundry TAM

growth of 22% YoY, as the company continues to focus on mid-to-high-end and
premium smartphone models (iOS camp accounts for 30-40% of the company’s cellular
business per our estimate), which are relatively more resilient to rising memory costs.
Moreover, we expect Win Semi’s PA foundry market share to decline from 74% in 2020
to 57% in 2026, and further lower to ~50% by 2030, but its revenue will continue to
expand further due to the growing foundry TAM.

We continue to expect Taiwan PA foundry players to see modest recovery in 2026
CAPEX. However, we do not view this as an indication of a recovery in demand for the
overall PA foundry industry. Win Semi maintained its 2026 CAPEX guidance of
NT$2-3bn, with the majority of spending allocated to the new InP and GaN capacity
rather than GaAs capacity expansion. We continue to believe GaAs capacity remains
sufficient as we estimate Win Semi’s quarterly utilization rate could reach at most 85%

throughout 2026-28E (Exhibit 1), suggesting no meaningful need for additional GaAs
capacity expansion.

Investment view 
The company has started to see increasing contribution from the new PD product in 
3Q26 but the contribution remains very limited (overall optical business only saw high 
single digit QoQ in 3Q26), while the company expects PD product revenue to further 
ramp up in 2027 along with more PD capacity coming online. This is tracking more slowly 
than our expectation, and we now expect the datacom optical business to account for 
only a mid-single-digit of 2026E revenue (was expecting high-single-digit previously). 
Meanwhile, progress in new high end LD products (including CW lasers, EML solutions 
and VCSEL) remains broadly the same since last earning call, with the company is still at 
verification stage and expect more meaningful contribution in 2028E. As such, we 
believe Win Semi’s revenue remains highly exposed to the potential weakness in 
smartphone demand, as we estimate smartphone related business (including cellular, 
WiFi, and optical) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2026E. The contribution

We maintain our conservative view on Win Semi’s stock performance, given (1) the
contribution from datacom optical products (e.g., PDs and LDs) remains limited in

near-term, with the higher margin LD products potentially seeing more contribution in
2028E, (2) the company’s exposure in cellular business remains high (30%+), and the
smartphone market continue to be impacted by high memory cost; and (3) given the
slow technology upgrade speed in the GaAs foundry market, we believe competitors
such as AWSC could catch up with the technology and is better positioned to gain share
given their lower cost structures and more competitive pricing.

Maintain Sell, with a new 12-m TP of NT$162 (from NT$142).

### Page 7

Earnings revisions  
We raise our 2026E revenue by 2% after factor in 3Q26 results and raise our 2027/28E 
revenue by 5/6% to factor in higher infrastructure and optical business contribution. We 
revise up our 2026/27/28E GM estimates by 3.8/2.1/1.6ppt as we expect more margin 
expansion driven by higher UTR and the higher contribution from high margin 
infrastructure business. Overall, we revised up our 2026/27/28E EPS estimates by 
47/6/7%. 
Also, we introduce our 2029E earnings estimates with this report.

Valuation 
We maintain our Sell rating, and revise up our TP from NT$142 to NT$162, based on the 
same target PE of 24x (1x STDV lower than the industry upcycle average valuation), 
factoring in our earnings revisions and, consistent with our group coverage practices, roll 
over our valuation period from 4Q26-3Q27 to 2027E.

Investment Thesis and TP & risks

Win Semi is a key GaAs-based semi foundry player with c.49% global market share as of 
2025. We believe the company’s market share could decline given its much 
higher-than-peer product pricing in the demand downtrend. We also expect the

company to be impacted by the slow PA foundry market growth outlook given weak

opportunity. We are Sell-rated given the ongoing PA foundry industry downcycle, along

above its average downcycle P/E multiple. Key upside risks are (1) easing competition in
the China PA foundry market, (2) faster-than-expected progress on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade
trend, and (3) faster-than-expected growth from LEO/AI-related business.

lower than the industry upcycle average valuation since 2016. Key upside risks are (1)
easing competition in the China PA foundry market, (2) faster-than-expected progress
on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade trend, and (3) faster-than-expected growth from LEO/AI-related
business.

## Document 116: 261029_ms_vanguard-DG

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_ms_vanguard-DG.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

M
Idea
VSMC could cause  high-single digit (%) dilution to 2027 GM – above many
investors’ 3-5% expectation: On Vanguard’s latest earnings call, management noted
a successful trial run of VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, its JV
with NXP Semiconductors. It confirmed that interposers are on track for mass
production  in 1Q27. We see revenue upside from the ramp – but depreciation could
heavily pressure margins. Management previously guided that VSMC could break

even at 30kwpm, more likely in late 2028, in our view (vs. current 8-inch margin at
30-35%). VSMC  capacity is scheduled at 44kwpm, ~100kwpm 8-inch equivalent,
about one-third of current 8-inch capacity (280kwpm). On the other hand, we think
new capacity may only run at <5kwpm in 1Q27 and <20kwpm by end-2027;
depreciation reflects 44kwpm (VSMC's full capacity) for infrastructure, clean room,
and construction. We therefore project that VSMC's gross margin dilution in full-

year 2027 could be more severe than many expect.
Despite strong customer commitment, VSMC could still dilute the corporate
margin average for at least the next five years: Since management previously
guided that VSMC could break even at 30kwpm, we view its break-even point as late
2028 or 1H29. It will then take years for GM to rise from 0% to 30-40%, even if the
capacity is fully booked. We thus expect a drag on corporate average GM for at least

the next five years. The good news: VSMC demand is skewed to high-margin
businesses –  interposers, industrial, and automotive – so the margin improvement
could be smoother  vs. peers like Hua Hong.
2027 might bring double-digit wafer price hikes and AI-related revenue at 20-
30% of the mix: During its latest earnings call, UMC guided for a 10% blended
wafer ASP increase in 2027. Given larger exposure to AI server PMIC, industrial, and
automotive, we expect Vanguard to raise 8-inch wafer prices 10-15% for the full year.

Given some customer pushback to front-loaded hikes, the price hike might be split –
twice a year, each 5-10%. Also, the company would still have L TAs expiring in 2027,
so we don’t expect aggressive wafer ASP guidance for 1Q27. With new interposer
production, we believe AI-related revenue could reach 20-30% of total revenue.
Down to UW from EW: The margin impact is larger than we initially expected and
could last 5-10 years.  Our new price target implies 2.8x our 2027e BVPS.

Idea
2
Vanguard could still show strong revenue/margin
performance for 2H26
4Q26 should maintain strong growth: We expect Vanguard to sustain strong
performance into 4Q26, supported by resilient demand from the industrial and
automotive end markets. In addition to a favorable demand outlook, ongoing capacity
optimization initiatives, particularly the transition from thicker-wire to thinner-wire
manufacturing processes, should enhance production efficiency, improve capacity

utilization, and support higher output. These operational improvements, coupled with a
favorable product mix and steady order momentum, could further underpin revenue
growth and profitability in 4Q26. We now expect Vanguard 8-inch process margin to reach
34-36% in both 4Q26 and 1Q27.

Key questions to ask during the upcoming earnings call on November 6:
1.  Price hike into 2027: How much of the incremental cost could Vanguard pass
through  to customers? Could we see double-digit apples-to-apples price hikes into
2027 despite the expiration of some L TAs?

2.  AI-related revenue: How much could  AI related revenue grow in 2027 if we
include both AI server PMIC and interposer production?
3.  Singapore fab – margin dilution and capacity allocation: How could  margin be
affected by VSMC in both 2027 and 2028? How much would depreciation be in
2027 and 2028? How would the company allocate 12-inch capacity between
interposers, NXP, and other customers?
4.  Consumer demand: Has the company seen any slowdown in consumer demand?

And how soon could that capacity be filled up by other sources of demand?
5.  Further capacity expansion: Might the company have further plans to expand 8-
inch capacity or second Singapore fab since the utilization rate has been reaching
>90%?

### Page 6

M
Idea
6
Valuation  methodology
We lower our price target (base case value) to NT$135 from NT$180:  We factor in our
earnings estimate changes for 2027-28 and also lower the intermediate growth rate
assumption from 13% to 10.5% to reflect the long-term margin dilution.
We continue to derive our price target from a residual income model, which we believe
best captures the stock's long-term value. Our key assumptions other than the
intermediate growth rate are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2,

equity risk premium of 6% and risk-free rate of 2%),  and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.
We also lower our bull and bear case values from NT$260 and NT$110 to NT$195 and NT
$80, accordingly, implying 4.0x and 1.7x our 2027e BVPS.

MS Rating
▪ We expect 2026 capex to increase beyond
2025's high level given Singapore fab

expansion, leading to long-term margin
pressure.
▪ We expect PMIC wafer price hikes in the
industry in response to strong demand from
AI. Vanguard could beneﬁt from TSMC
mature node consolidation as well as
interposer production.
▪ The new Singapore fab could cause high-
single-digit margin dilution to the corporate
average in 2027.
▪ VSMC's 12-inch capacity is all fully booked;
production will start to ramp in 2027 with
depreciation pressure during the early stage.

▪ Our UW rating is mostly because of the
margin pressure.

NT$195.00
4.0x base case 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi demand: Auto semi demand is
exceptionally strong; inventory is digested
quickly, and DDI for smartphones and large

panels shows robust growth. We forecast a
>30% revenue CAGR in 2025-28.
Competition from peers eases. We assume:
1) global semi sales growth of >20% and
foundry industry growth of >15% in 2026; 2)
better-than-expected demand for 8"
capacity; and 3) power demand for 5G
tracking ahead of schedule.
NT$135.00
2.8x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth slowdown: Demand has
started to pick up for LDDIC and PMIC,
while auto semis inventory digestion is
reaching an end. We forecast a 25% revenue

CAGR in 2025-28. Vanguard starts to
reverse pricing discounts to customers in
view of stronger demand and some rush
orders in the short run. We assume: 1) global
semi sales show growth in 2026; and 2) the
foundry industry shows single-digit growth
in 2026.
NT$80.00
1.7x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth decelerates: Auto semi demand
weakens markedly; power semi inventory
levels remain elevated. Smartphone DDI
shows a worse-than-expected decline.
Intensiﬁed competition and new Chinese

capacity dampen the overall pricing
environment. We forecast <10% revenue
CAGR in 2025-28. We assume: 1) overall
semi industry continues to decelerate in
2026 and foundry growth is stagnant; and 2)
demand for 8" capacity is weaker than
expected owing to faster product migration.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 238: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 248: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다. 
목표주가 (하향) 2,000,000원  
현재가 (10/6) 1,310,000원

PolyPeptide 인수 2.71 2026년 CHF 1,462,957,171.41 기준 
6공장 증설 1.9 2027~2029년 180kL Capa 확장 투자 (바이오리액터, 정제설비 등) 
제3바이오캠퍼스 증설 7.0 2027~2034년 주력 항체 이외 차세대 Modality 확장 관련 생산 설비 도입

당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

## Document 261: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2 분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2 분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4 분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3%

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINTS

per year

(AI)

 The middle class is still modest
 . This is explained by a large 
informal economy, heavy concentration of employment in low value

 India faces two key structural challenges: i

Even with progress in sectors such as smartphones and pharmaceuticals, India’s industrial base remains too small to 
absorb its vast labour force. 
 Labo

 despite

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE

Non transfer risk   
India’s external accounts are solid with a low external debt, moderate current account deficit and large FX 
reserves. 
 India’s net debtor position reached only 5.9% of GDP in Q2 2026. In the short term, external accounts 
are expected to deteriorate 
 given 
the rise in energy prices while downward pressures on the rupee should remain 
high.  
◼ 
 External accounts have remained solid 
 despite recent adverse shocks
 .

 H1

exchange reserves should be more than sufficient to cover the co
 untry’s short
 -
 term financing needs. The 
 major  
causes 
for concern are the 
 structurally

portfolio investment, which exposes the currency to greater volatility.

rise in energy prices especially thanks to the sharp increase in workers’ remittances  
(+0.7 pp to 3.7% of GDP)

Systemic credit risk  
The banking sector has strengthened over the past five years
 , 
as illustrated by the improvement in 
 asset  
quality 
and capital buffers. The main sources of credit risks are rising household debt, the rise in energy prices, the 
unfavourable monsoon and monetary tightening.  
◼ 
 The quality of assets has improved significantly. 
 In Q1 2026, the banking sector’s non
 -
 performing loan (NPL) ratio fell 
to a multi
 -
 decade low of 1.8%. For public banks  
(
54.4% of total loans
 )
, the NPL ratio declined to 2.1%.  
◼ 
 Capital buffers are strong. 
 While 
loan loss provisions remained modest at 75.6% of NPLs, capital buffers stayed strong. 
The Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) rose to 17.7% in Q1 2026, up from 17.4% a year earlier. From April 2027, Indian banks 
will adopt a new Expected Credit Loss (ECL) framewo
 rk, requiring earlier provisioning for potential loan losses. In the 
short term, higher provisioning requirements could push up lending rates and tighten credit conditions 
 while 
 triggering 
capital raising or targeted government recapitalisation. 
 I
n the medium term, the reform could strengthen banking  
sector 
resilience through earlier recognition of credit risks and more robust loss buffers.  
◼ 
 Banks’ 
 liquidity 
 is 
 comfortable
 . 
It deteriorated slightly in H1 2026 because credit growth outpaced deposit growth
 ; t
he 
liquidity coverage ratio decli
 ned to 
a still solid 
 124% 
and 
the loan
 -

-

a year earlier. RBI interventions to support the rupee 
 also 
 drained liquidity
 . Moreover
 , the deposit mix shifted from low
 -
cost current and savings accounts (CASA) to higher
 -

June 2026, liquidity has improved significantly due to RBI measures to attract foreign
 -
 currency non
 -

sident (FCNR) 
deposits.  
◼ 
 Profitability remains satisfactory. 
 It 
 has declined since the start of the year, primarily due to narrowing net interest 
margins, but remains satisfactory. In Q2 2026, 
 the 
 return on assets (ROA) and return on equity (ROE) stood at 1.6% and 
13.0%, respectively (IMF data).  
◼ 
 P
rivate
 -
 sector  
 debt 
 is moderate
 , 
 but its increase 
 needs to be monitored.  
 It stood 
 at 105.1% of GDP in Q1 2026 (BIS  
data
), compared to 100.1% five years earlier
 , which is 
 lower than in other emerging Asian economies.  
Non
-
 financial corporat
 e 
debt was 56% of GDP in Q1 2026. Listed firms’ financial positions continued to strengthen amid 
surging profits, improving their ability to service debt. In Q2 2026, the 
 average 
 interest coverage ratio (ICR) rose to 7.4, up 
from 6.1 a year earlier. However, the share of companies with an ICR below 1 (insufficient earnings to cover interest costs) 
increased to 18.5%. Stress remain
 s 
 concentrated in micro
 -
 enterprises, agriculture, and energy
 -
 intensive sectors (
 e.g.
, 
power, steel, cement, aviation), which are 
 the 
most exposed to oil
 -

Household leverage requires monitoring. Although still moderate, household debt increased to 49.1% of GDP in Q1 2026, 
up from 42.7% five years earlier, driven largely by non
 -

◼ 
 Monetary 
 policy 
 tightening. 
 Due to rising  
inflation (+5.5% year
 -
 on
-
 year in September) and downward pressure on the 
rupee, the RBI raised its policy rate by 25 basis points to 5.5% in October 2026 under a "calibrated tightening" stance.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector should remain resilient  
 despite rising risks in the short term. 
 Due to rising energy prices, a poor 
monsoon, and monetary tightening, asset quality is expected to deteriorate, particularly for low
 -

and small enterprises. 
 E
arly warning signs are emerging
 , with some

concentrated in unsecured retail loans  
(
which account for over half of fresh retail slippages
 )
. 
This could lead to new 
 NPLs 
over the coming quarters, especially as higher rates and inflation squeeze repayment capacity. Additionally, in the near 
term, capital inflows may remain weak, 
 and

## Document 340: Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports
Europe’s auto deal with China is a good start. But containing China’s export boom
will require a much broader response.
European Commissioner for Trade and Economic Security Maros Sefcovic speaks at a press briefing at the EU Delegation to
China in Beijing, China October 9, 2026. Florence Lo/Reuters
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
November 2, 2026 11:02 a.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The year-over-year increase in Chinese automobile
exports to all markets is currently about 3.5 million
cars.
Over ten years, that is 35 million cars. The global
market excluding China is only around 60 million
cars. Japan raised its auto exports by about 3.5 million
cars in the 1970s—but that was over a decade, not
one year. And right now the U.S. and Indian markets

(around 20 million cars) are effectively closed to
Chinese production. The 2026 jump in exports is an
incredible.
China’s domestic market, though, is down 20 percent
this year—that works out to a decline of about 5
million cars. The Wall Street Journal estimates that
domestic sales will fall to around 19 million cars this
year, less than a third of the production capacity of
China’s auto industry.
So even though China’s exports are increasing at an

unprecedented pace, China’s domestic auto sector
isn’t really booming; production is essentially flat, and
intense domestic competition is destroying the
profitability of firms that don’t export.*

### Page 3

3
Europe’s new auto deal with China should be
understood in this context.
The deal limits China’s exports of hybrids (including
plug-in hybrids) to Europe to around 350,000 cars.
That is above the 300,000 hybrids that China
exported to Europe in 2025, but well below the (gulp)
800,000 annualized pace for hybrids in the last three
months of (European) data. Total imports,
annualized, were running at an incredible 2 million a
year pace thanks to strong battery electric imports in

September.

### Page 4

4
China appears to have concluded that it prefers a
guaranteed market for a limited number of cars (and
more or less guaranteed profits) to the risk of a
prohibitive tariff that would cut off all its exports.
And Europe preferred a negotiated deal to the risk of
retaliation and a slowdown in licensing rare earth and
magnet exports.
The Commission also thinks of this as a bit of a
model—more on that later.
I certainly understand the logic of the deal.

Something needed to be done. China’s export of
hybrids to Europe were up an insane 200 percent
year-over-year in the last 3 months of data, which led
to a 75 percent increase for all cars. One more year of
75 percent growth would bring China’s exports to

### Page 5

5
around 3 million cars; in two years, close to 6 million,
and in three years, 10 million—or most of the market.
But Europe still is a long way from a coherent trade
and industrial policy with respect to autos, let alone a
coherent economic policy towards overall trade with
China.
After this deal, as I understand it:
Hybrid imports are capped at 350,000, down
from a projected 800,000 in 2026—and then
rising to 370,000 and 390,000 in subsequent
years.

Battery electric vehicles face a tariff from 25
percent (Telsa, 10 percent base + 15 percent) to
45 percent (10 percent base plus 35 percent).
BYD’s total tariff is at 27 percent, which isn’t
prohibitive. Even with these tariffs, battery
electric imports will total around 600,000 this
year, up from 500,000 last year.
Diesel and gasoline (ICE) cars face a tariff of
10 percent. Europe is importing around
200,000 of these a year (including a significant

number from Chinese-owned MG).
There are no special tariffs on imported parts or
imported batteries, which matters for the growing
number of Chinese factories in Europe. So long as
tariffs are low and the renminbi is undervalued,
Chinese firms still have an incentive to maximize the

### Page 6

6
Chinese content of their “transplant” factories (ask
Thailand).
There is currently no unified European policy on
inward investment that requires joint ventures or
technology transfer either, only a lot of wishful
thinking.**
Sum it up, and it is a market where Chinese exports
are constrained by a hodge-podge of different trade
defense tools, and there is no standard policy for
ensuring that Chinese assembly operations use
European parts rather than import Chinese kits. The

existing operations, which are just starting to scale up,
don’t draw on a European supply chain. They likely
preserve a few assembly jobs at the expense of the
broader automotive ecosystem.
A coherent European (or national) industrial policy
for the automotive and battery sectors is thus still a
work in progress.
The safeguard on hybrids and the resulting tariff rate
quota only stops the bleeding.
And make no mistake, Europe’s position in the global

automotive sector is slipping. Europe still runs an
overall surplus in auto trade, but that surplus is falling
fast if trade in batteries is added to automotive trade
(there isn’t a split between batteries used for energy

### Page 7

7
storage and batteries used by cars in the EU data
right now, at least not that I have found).
Europe’s broader trade policy to China is similarly a
work in progress.
The surge in imports of hybrid cars was particularly
pronounced, but it is by no means the only such surge.
The balance in industrial machinery has eroded
significantly. Consider the following chart, which
shows imports and exports of machinery, electronics
and vehicles (HS 84, 85, and 87) by weight.

### Page 8

8
The balance in chemicals, measured by weight to
abstract from price changes, shows the same trend.
Europe doesn’t have an across-the-board tool for
applying tariffs against China like the U.S. section
301 statute.

### Page 9

9
But that isn’t necessarily an immediate constraint, as
there are plenty of solid safeguard cases thanks to the
current surge in European imports from China.
But if each safeguard needs to include a negotiated
tariff-rate quota that gives China enough guaranteed
market access (and Chinese firms enough guaranteed
profits) to ensure trade peace, Europe needs to get
moving with negotiation in other sectors.
Chemicals and machinery are more diverse sectors

than cars and thus aren’t as amenable to the same
high-level Š ef č ovi č -Wang negotiations the led to the
most recent deal.
This critique isn’t meant as criticism. Europe needed
to act on cars. Look at a chart of automotive trade in
kilos.

### Page 10

10
It’s just that it also needs to act in other sectors: auto
parts, batteries (though that is complicated),
chemicals, and some categories of industrial
machinery. The scale of China’s export boom and the
fact that in most sectors there are no limits to China’s
ability to scale up is a real problem.***
And Europe cannot wait for China to conclude that
it would rather grow on the basis of internal, rather
than external, demand.
I disagree with the IMF’s former top brass on this. A

China that grows on the back of a 1.5 pp net export
contribution for another couple of years will
deindustrialize much of the world. China’s trading
partners cannot step aside and do nothing until
China figures out how to escape deflation using its
own demand. Xi appears more than happy to
continue with his current policy mix and gamble that

### Page 11

11
the world will continue to absorb China’s ever
growing export surplus.
Pascal Lamy, Nicolas Köhler-Suzuki, and Victor Luiz
do Prado of the Jacques Delors Institute recognize
the need for a more systemic response—as do Jean-
Pierre Landau and Sébastien Jean, who have done an
excellent job explaining how China’s micro- and
macro-industrial policies fit together into a coherent,
self-reinforcing whole.
The proposals in the Lamy et al. paper overlap heavily

with the Commission’s current approach to China.
The authors envision a two-pronged approach: first,
negotiating with China to seek agreement on tariff-
rate quotas that effectively limit the EU’s imports
from China; and second, developing a broad yet
targeted tariff-based instrument to address structural
imbalances should negotiations fail. The latter would
give Europe a credible threat should China refuse to
agree to the proposed safeguards.
My own view: don’t let the perfect be the enemy of

the good.
The EU has shied away from developing its own 301
tool out of concern about preserving a “rules-based
system.” I still think a European 301 would allow a
more fulsome response, but understand that
European isn’t as willing as the United States to use

### Page 12

12
tools that at least appear to be outside those
authorized by the WTO. In reality the EU no longer
really wants to trade with China on the same (MFN)
terms applied to other WTO members. That mirrors
the current view of the United States. But the EU is
willing to go through more procedural hoops to get to
a similar result.****
But I also recognize that, absent policy adjustment in
China—notably a stronger currency that allows for
fair market competition—the scope of safeguards and

managed trade will progressively expand.
* One sign: Stellantis is resuming Jeep production in
China, but primarily for export rather than the
domestic market.
** There is a lot of (in my view) somewhat confused
analysis of Chinese investment that works off the
analogy provided by either foreign investment in
China’s auto sector or Japanese investment in the U.S.
and Europe in the 1980s. These are relevant examples
of course, but asserting that Chinese investment will

result in a similar outcome when the underlying
economics differ is a big logical jump. Japanese firms
had incentives to localize their parts production in the
United States from the then-strong yen as well as
from the risk of protection. Foreign firms operating in
China had to do JVs until recently and had incentives
to localize from China’s significant tariff on auto

### Page 13

13
parts and China’s lower cost structure (in part because
of a weak RMB). Tesla’s Shanghai incentives and tax
deal included strong localization incentives, and there
were additional incentives from Tesla’s desire to
qualify for the lists the governed access to China’s
consumer EV subsidies. That is why something like
90 percent of Tesla parts, including its batteries, are
now made in China. Chinese investors in Europe
have no similar incentives to localize right now, yet

many assume that the results will be the same. That,
to me, is naïve. Since the underlying economics differ
(Europe’s cost basis is much higher than existing
production in China) getting Chinese firms to do
more than final assembly in Europe will take stronger
policy tools than were required to get foreign firms to
localize parts production in China. Europe’s
Industrial Accelerator Act would be a step in the
right direction, but more is likely needed. Thailand

provides a useful cautionary example.
*** China can make over 60 million cars, according to
recent reporting from the Wall Street Journal. The
domestic market this year will be under 20 million
cars (down from something like 24 million cars last
year). Even with exports of 10 million, there is ~30
million cars worth of unused auto production
capacity (roughly double European demand). And
China is still investing at a pace that is consistent
with adding close to 5 million of EV production

capacity a year. Chinese firms, who don’t make any
money on domestic sales, are strongly incentivized to

## Document 363: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP , household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.
